\|S_{A}(u)\|_{L^{q}}\lesssim C\|N(\nabla u)\|_{L^{q}}
for all
q>1
follows.

We further decompose
S_{A}(u)(P)=S_{A,<r}(u)(P)+S_{A,\geq r}(u)(P)
, where
S_{A,<r}
is the truncated version of
S_{A}
using cones of height
r=\mbox{diam}(\Delta_{i})
, with
S_{A,\geq r}
being the complementary term. We aim to prove that
S_{A,<r}(u)(P)>\lambda
for all
P\in\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}
, provided
\gamma
is chosen small enough.
Assuming this for the moment we have that

(6.6)

|\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}}S_{A,<r}^{2}(u).

By Fubini, the first term enjoys the estimate

\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}}S_{A,<r}^{2}(u)dx\,dt\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\iint_{\cup\{\Gamma^{r}(P):\,P\in\Delta_{i}\bigcap E_{\lambda}\}}(|\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{2}+|\nabla_{\parallel}A|^{2})|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}\,dX\,dt

\leq\gamma^{2}\iint_{T(2\Delta_{i})}(|\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{2}+|\nabla_{\parallel}A|^{2})x_{n}\,dX\,dt\leq C\gamma^{2}\|\mu_{\parallel}\|_{C}|\Delta_{i}|,

using the Carleson condition and the fact that on the domain of integration
N(\nabla u)\leq\gamma\lambda
.

It remains to show that
S_{A,<r}(u)(P)>\lambda
for all
P\in\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}
.
We use the fact that
2\Delta_{i}
is not contained in
E_{\lambda}
and hence for some
Q\in 2\Delta_{i}\setminus\Delta_{i}
we have that
S_{A}(u)(Q)\leq\lambda
.
It follows that for all
P\in\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}

(6.7)

\mathcal{I}:=\iint_{\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(P)\setminus\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(Q)}(|\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{2}+|\nabla_{\parallel}A|^{2})|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}^{-n}\,dX\,dt\geq 3\lambda^{2}.

We consider the above integral of
\mathcal{I}
over the set
(\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(P)\setminus\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(Q))\cap\{\delta(X,t)\geq r\}
, which
we denote
\mathcal{I}^{top}
, and
\mathcal{I}^{bottom}
will be the difference. We further split
\mathcal{I}^{top}
dyadically into regions where
x_{n}\approx 2^{k}r
,
k\geq 1
to get

\mathcal{I}^{top}\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}\iint_{(\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(P)\setminus\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(Q))\cap\{x_{n}\approx 2^{k}r\}}(|\partial_{t}A|^{2}x_{n}^{2}+|\nabla_{\parallel}A|^{2})|\nabla u|^{2}x_{n}^{-n}\,dX\,dt

\leq\sum_{k\geq 1}|(\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(P)\setminus\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(Q))\cap\{x_{n}\approx 2^{k}r\}|(2^{k}r)^{-n-2}\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2},

where we have used the trivial pointwise bounds
|\nabla_{\parallel}A|\lesssim x_{n}^{-1}
and
|\partial_{t}A|\lesssim x_{n}^{-2}
leaving us with
N(\nabla u)(P)
, which
is bounded by
\gamma\lambda
for
P\in E_{\lambda}
.

It remains to estimate the volume of the set
(\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(P)\setminus\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(Q))\cap\{x_{n}\approx 2^{k}r\}
. Being a difference of two cones with vertices of distance
\leq r
, this set has at least one “short side”
which does not grow as
2^{k}r
and instead has size
r
for each
k
. It follows that

|(\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(P)\setminus\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(Q))\cap\{x_{n}\approx 2^{k}r\}|\lesssim(2^{k}r)^{n+1}r=(2^{k})^{n+1}r^{n+2}.

Using this and the estimate above we have that

\mathcal{I}^{top}\leq C\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}(2^{k})^{n+1}r^{n+2}(2^{k}r)^{-n-2}=C\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}\sum_{k\geq 1}2^{-k}=C\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}.

Hence we conclude that for
P\in\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}
we have that

\mathcal{I}^{bottom}=\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{I}^{top}\geq 3\lambda^{2}-C\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}=(3-C\gamma^{2})\lambda^{2}.

We chose
\gamma>0
such that
(3-C\gamma^{2})>1
and then we get that

S_{A,<r}(u)(P)^{2}\geq\mathcal{I}^{bottom}>\lambda^{2},

for all
P\in\Delta_{i}\cap E_{\lambda}
as desired. Hence we have established (
6.5
).∎

7.
Estimates for
D^{1/2}_{t}u

7.1.
The PDE for
D^{1/2}_{t}u
.

In this section, we find the partial differential equation that
D^{1/2}_{t}u
satisfies, where
u
is a weak solution of (
1.1
). Define
w:=D^{1/2}_{t}u
.

Lemma 7.1
.

The function
w
satisfies

(7.2)

\partial_{t}w(X,t)=\divg(A\nabla w)(X,t)+c_{0}\divg\left(\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)-A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\right).

Proof:
We can assume that
A
is smooth, which is valid by the reasoning in Remark
3.17
(3). We can also assume that
u
is a Schwartz function, as these are dense in the solution space.

Taking the Fourier transform in
t
, we have

\partial_{t}w(X,\cdot)^{\wedge}(\tau)=i\tau D^{1/2}_{t}u(X,\cdot)^{\wedge}(\tau)=\left|\tau\right|^{1/2}\partial_{t}u(X,\cdot)^{\wedge}(\tau).

Taking the Fourier transform in
t
on both sides of the PDE for
u
gives that

\partial_{t}u(X,\cdot)^{\wedge}(\tau)=\divg\left((A(X,\cdot)\nabla u(X,\cdot))^{\wedge}(\tau)\right).

Plugging this into the previous equation yields that

\widehat{\partial_{t}w(\tau)}=\left|\tau\right|^{1/2}\divg\left((A\nabla u)^{\wedge}(\tau)\right)=\divg\left((D^{1/2}_{t}(A\nabla u))^{\wedge}(\tau)\right),

where we have omitted
X
for simplicity,
and hence,

(7.3)

\partial_{t}w(X,t)=\divg\left(D^{1/2}_{t}(A\nabla u)(X,t)\right).

On the other hand,

c_{0}^{-1}D^{1/2}_{t}(A(X,t)\nabla u(X,t))=\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)\nabla u(X,t)-A(X,s)\nabla u(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}ds\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)(\nabla u(X,t)-\nabla u(X,s))}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(A(X,t)-A(X,s))\nabla u(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}ds\\
=c_{0}^{-1}A(X,t)D^{1/2}_{t}(\nabla u)(X,t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(A(X,t)-A(X,s))\nabla u(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}ds\\
=c_{0}^{-1}A(X,t)\nabla w(X,t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(A(X,t)-A(X,s))\nabla u(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}ds.

Plugging this back into (
7.3
) gives (
7.2
).

\square

7.2.
Caccioppoli-type estimates for
\nabla^{2}D^{1/2}_{t}u
.

We will need a Caccioppoli-type estimate for the second derivatives of
D^{1/2}_{t}u
, where
u
is a weak solution of (
1.1
). Recall that
w=D^{1/2}_{t}u
satisfies the equation (
7.2
).

Since the equation contains a non-local term with respect to the time variable, we cannot hope for local estimates in time. Fortunately, we only need to localize the spatial variables in the Caccioppoli-type estimate in application. Let us denote the spatial cube centered at
Y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
with sidelength
r
by
Q_{r}(Y)
; that is,

Q_{r}(Y)=\left\{X\in\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}:\,\left|x_{i}-y_{i}\right|<r\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq n\right\}.

Lemma 7.4

(A Caccioppoli inequality for
\nabla^{2}D^{1/2}_{t}u
)
.

Suppose that
A
satisfies (
1.2
) and (
1.4
). Let
w
be a solution of (
7.2
). Let
Y=(y,y_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
and
r>0
be such that
Q_{2r}=Q_{2r}(Y)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
.
Then

(7.5)

\int_{Q_{r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}dtdX\lesssim\frac{1}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}dtdX+\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\frac{dtdX}{x_{n}^{2}}\\
+\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\frac{dtdX}{x_{n}^{2}}+\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{x_{n}^{2}}(\partial_{\tau}\nabla A(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}x_{n}^{2}dtdX\\
+\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}u(X,t)\right|^{2}\frac{dtdX}{x_{n}^{2}},

where
M_{x_{n}^{2}}
is the localized maximal function at scale
x_{n}^{2}
as defined in (
3.45
).

Proof:
Let
1\leq k\leq n
be fixed. We differentiate the PDE (
7.2
) with respect to
x_{k}
and take
\varphi_{k}=(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}
as a test function, where
\zeta=\zeta(X)\in[0,1]
is a smooth cutoff function equal to 1 on
Q_{r}(Y)
and supported in
Q_{2r}(Y)
satisfying
r\left|\nabla\zeta\right|\leq c
for some
c>0
. It follows that

\iint\partial_{t}\partial_{k}w(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}dtdX=-\iint(\partial_{k}A)\nabla w\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right)-\iint A\nabla\partial_{k}w\cdot\nabla(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w)\\
-\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right)\\
-\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)-A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla(\partial_{k}u)(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right).

Since
\zeta
is
t
-independent, the left-hand side above is equal to

\frac{1}{2}\iint\partial_{t}\left((\partial_{k}w)^{2}\zeta^{2}\right)dtdX=0.

This implies that

\iint A\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\cdot\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}=-\iint(\partial_{k}A)\nabla w\cdot\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}-2\iint(\partial_{k}A)\nabla w\cdot\nabla\zeta(\partial_{k}w)\zeta\\
-2\iint A\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\cdot\nabla\zeta(\partial_{k}w)\zeta-\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right)\\
-\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)-A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla(\partial_{k}u)(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right).

Note that the assumption that
Q_{2r}(Y)\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
entails that
y_{n}>2r
, and thus
\left|\nabla A\right|\leq Cr^{-1}
in
Q_{2r}
(see (
3.36
)). By ellipticity and applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the first three terms on the right-hand side, we get that

(7.6)

\lambda\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}\leq\frac{\lambda}{4}\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}+\frac{C}{r^{2}}\int_{Q_{2r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}dtdX+I+J,

where

I:=\left|\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right)\right|,

and

J:=\left|\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)-A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla(\partial_{k}u)(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right)\right|.

We only need to focus on
I
and
J
. For both of them, we split the integral in
s
into regions
\left\{s:\,\left|t-s\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}\right\}
and
\left\{s:\,\left|t-s\right|>x_{n}^{2}\right\}
,
obtaining four terms in total.

We start with the terms from
I
.
We claim that

(7.7)

\left|I_{11}\right|:=\left|\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}dtdX\right|\\
\leq\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\frac{\zeta(X)^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
+\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}dtdX\right)^{1/2}

To see this, use the triangle inequality and bound the left-hand side of (
7.7
) by

\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\partial_{k}A(X,t)\right|}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\left|\nabla u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX\\
+\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\partial_{k}A(X,s)\right|}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\left|\nabla u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX=:I_{111}+I_{112}

For
I_{112}
, use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the
L^{2}
boundedness of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function:

I_{112}\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}\iint\int_{2^{j}x_{n}^{2}\leq\left|s-t\right|\leq 2^{j+1}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\partial_{k}A(X,s)\right|}{\left(2^{j}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{2/3}}\left|\nabla u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX\\
\leq\iint\sum_{j\geq 0}\left(2^{j}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{-1/2}M\left(\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\nabla u(X,\cdot)\right)(t)\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX\\
\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\frac{\zeta(X)}{x_{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}M(\partial_{k}A\nabla u(X,\cdot))^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX\\
\lesssim\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}\,dtdX\right)^{1/2}.

The term
I_{111}
can be treated similarly:

I_{111}\leq\sum_{j\geq 0}\iint\int_{2^{j}x_{n}^{2}\leq\left|s-t\right|\leq 2^{j+1}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\partial_{k}A(X,t)\right|}{\left(2^{j}x_{n}^{2}\right)^{2/3}}\left|\nabla u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX\\
\lesssim\iint\left|\partial_{k}A(X,t)\right|M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta(X)^{2}x_{n}^{-1}dtdX\\
\leq\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}\,dtdX\right)^{1/2},

which gives (
7.7
).
The other term from
I
, when
\left|t-s\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}
, is

I_{12}:=\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\zeta(\partial_{k}w)\zeta\,dtdX.

It is straightforward to check that, using a similar argument,

(7.8)

\left|I_{12}\right|\leq\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\frac{\zeta(X)^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\left|\nabla\zeta\right|^{2}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
+\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\left|\nabla\zeta\right|^{2}dtdX\right)^{1/2}.

Now we turn to the terms from
I
when
\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}
. We write

I_{21}:=\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}dtdX\\
=\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{\tau}\partial_{k}A(X,\tau)d\tau}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\zeta^{2}dtdX.

Using the localized maximal function defined as in (
3.45
) gives

\left|I_{21}\right|\leq 2\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{1}{\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}}\fint_{s-\left|s-t\right|}^{s+\left|s-t\right|}\left|\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\tau)\right|d\tau\left|\nabla u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX\\
\leq 2\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-1/2}M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\right)(s)\left|\nabla u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta^{2}dtdX

Decompose the interval
\left\{s:\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}\right\}\subset\bigcup_{j\geq 0}\left\{s:2^{-j-1}x_{n}^{2}\leq\left|s-t\right|\leq 2^{-j}x_{n}^{2}\right\}
to get
an upper bound for
\left|I_{21}\right|

C\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-j/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\fint_{2^{-j-1}x_{n}^{2}\leq\left|s-t\right|\leq 2^{-j}x_{n}^{2}}M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\right)(s)\left|\nabla u\right|ds\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|dtdX\\
\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\zeta^{2}(X)x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}M\left(M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\right)\left|\nabla u(X,\cdot)\right|\right)(t)\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|dtdX.

By Cauchy-Schwarz and the
L^{2}
boundedness of the maximal function (in
t
), we find that

(7.9)

\left|I_{21}\right|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}{M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\right)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla u(X,t)\right|^{2}}dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX\\
\lesssim\left(\iint M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\right)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{2}\zeta(X)^{2}dXdt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta(X)^{2}dXdt\right)^{1/2}.

The only remaining term from
I
is

I_{22}:=\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\partial_{k}A(X,t)-\partial_{k}A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\zeta(\partial_{k}w)\zeta\,dtdX,

which can be estimated almost identically, and we see that
I_{21}
is bounded by

(7.10)

\left(\iint M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\partial_{k}A(X,\cdot)\right)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{2}\zeta(X)^{2}dXdt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\left|\nabla\zeta\right|^{2}dXdt\right)^{1/2}.

It remains to estimate

J:=\left|\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)-A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla(\partial_{k}u)(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\partial_{k}w\right)dtdX\right|\\
\leq\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\dots\right|+\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\dots\right|=:J_{1}+J_{2}.

For
J_{2}
, since

A(X,t)-A(X,s)=\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{\tau}A(X,\tau)d\tau
, the estimate
\left|\partial_{t}A(X,t)\right|\leq Cx_{n}^{-2}
implies

\left|A(X,t)-A(X,s)\right|\leq Cx_{n}^{-2}\left|t-s\right|.

Therefore,

J_{2}\leq C\iint x_{n}^{-2}\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,s)\right|}{\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}}ds\left(\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\right|\zeta^{2}+\left|\nabla\zeta\right|\left|\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta\right)dtdX.

Break the integral in
s
dyadically, and use the maximal function, to obtain:

J_{2}\leq C\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-j}\iint x_{n}^{-1}\fint_{\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{-j}x_{n}^{2}}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,s)\right|ds\left(\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\right|\zeta^{2}+\left|\nabla\zeta\right|\left|\partial_{k}w\right|\zeta\right)dtdX\\
\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\zeta(X)x_{n}^{-1}\int_{t\in R}M(\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,\cdot))(t)\left(\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}w)\right|\zeta+\left|\nabla\zeta\right|\left|\partial_{k}w\right|\right)dtdX.

By Cauchy-Schwarz and the
L^{2}
boundedness of the maximal function, we obtain that

J_{2}\leq C\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,t)\right|^{2}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{x_{n}^{2}}dXdt\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\bigg\{\left(\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}\,dXdt\right)^{1/2}+\left(\iint\left|\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\left|\nabla\zeta\right|^{2}\,dXdt\right)^{1/2}\bigg\}.

For
J_{1}
, Schur’s test shows that

\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,s)\right|\,ds\right)^{2}dt\leq x_{n}^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,t)\right|^{2}dt

as
\int_{\left|s-t\right|>x_{n}^{2}}\left|s-t\right|^{-3/2}ds=4x_{n}^{-1}
. Hence, apply Cauchy-Schwarz, we get that

J_{1}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\zeta(X)x_{n}^{-1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}u(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\zeta^{2}\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}+\left|\nabla\zeta\right|^{2}\left|\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX,

which has the same upper bound as
J_{2}
by Cauchy-Schwarz once again.

In conclusion, (
7.5
) follows from combining all the previous estimates, applying Young’s inequality, and choosing a sufficient small coefficient of
\iint\left|\nabla\partial_{k}w\right|^{2}\zeta^{2}
.

8.
S<N
estimates in
L^{2}
for block form matrix
A

In this section, we focus on the block form case when the matrix
A
is

(8.1)

A=\begin{bmatrix}\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\vbox{}\hrule width=0.0pt,height=0.0pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{}}}\hss}\hbox to0.0pt{\vbox{}\hss}\vbox{}}}\end{bmatrix}

and
A_{\parallel}=(a_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq n-1}
. Then
L=\divg A\nabla=\divg_{x}A_{\parallel}\nabla_{x}+\partial^{2}_{nn}
. The goal of this section is to prove the following result.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+L
be a parabolic operator with matrix
A
in the block form (
8.1
) in
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
A
satisfies the ellipticity condition (
1.2
), the Carleson condition (
1.3
) and the bound (
1.4
). Assume in addition that
\left|\partial_{t}\nabla A\right|\delta^{3}\in CM(\left\|\mu\right\|_{C})
.
Then there exist constants
C>0
and
K_{0}\geq 1
depending only on the dimension and the ellipticity constants, such that for any
f\in\dot{L}^{2}_{1,1/2}(\partial\Omega)
and any
K\geq K_{0}
,
the energy solution
u
to (
2.27
) satsifies

(8.3)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\leq C\left(K^{5}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+K^{-1}\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\right)\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{L}_{1,1/2}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2},

where
\mu_{||}
and
\mu
are defined in (
1.5
) and (
1.3
), respectively.

8.1.
Outline of the proof

We give an outline of our proof of Theorem
8.2
.

Step 1.
We obtain the
L^{2}
estimate for the square function of
\nabla_{x}u
: for
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1\leq i\leq n-1
,

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla(\partial_{i}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla_{x}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2},

which is a consequence of Lemma
8.5
.

Step 2.
We use the PDE of
u
and the block-form structure to obtain an
L^{2}
estimate for
\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt
, which is left out in Step 1.

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla_{x}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt.

This is proven in (
8.14
). So it remains to control
\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt
.

Step 3.
We derive an
L^{2}
estimate for the square function of
D_{t}^{1/2}u
: for any
K\geq 1
,

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla D_{t}^{1/2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\left(K^{4}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+K^{-2}\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\right)\left\|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{L}^{2}_{1,1/2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

This is shown in Lemma
8.15
.

Step 4.
We relate
\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt
to the square function of
D_{t}^{1/2}u
: for any
\epsilon>0
small,

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq\epsilon\iint\left|\nabla^{2}(D_{t}^{1/2}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt+C\left(1+\epsilon^{-1}\right)\iint\left|\nabla(D^{1/2}_{t}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}\\
+C\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C(1+\epsilon^{-1})\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

This is Lemma
8.24
.

Step 5.
We use a Caccioppoli type inequality (Lemma
7.4
) to control the first term on the right-hand side of the equation in Step 4:

\iint\left|\nabla^{2}(D_{t}^{1/2}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt\leq C\iint\left|\nabla(D^{1/2}_{t}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C\iint\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\\
+C\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

This is shown in Lemma
8.27
.

Step 6.
We plug the estimate from Step 5 into the estimate from Step 4, and we separate
\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|
from
\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|
because we know how to treat
\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\iint\left|\nabla(\partial_{i}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt
from Step 1. We obtain that for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

\iint\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq\epsilon\,C\iint\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C\left(1+\epsilon^{-1}\right)\iint\left|\nabla(D^{1/2}_{t}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\\
+C\epsilon^{-1}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+\epsilon\,C\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left\|\nabla_{x}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

Plugging this estimate into Step 2, using the estimate from Step 3, and then rearranging the coefficients yields that there is
C>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and any
K\geq 1
,

(8.4)

\iint\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq\epsilon\,C\iint\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{L}_{1,1/2}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}\\
+C\left(\epsilon^{-1}K^{4}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+\epsilon^{-1}K^{-2}\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\right)\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

Step 7.
We let
\epsilon=K^{-1}
and take
K_{0}
large enough so that
CK_{0}^{-1}<1/2
, and so for
K\geq K_{0}
, the first term on the right-hand side of (
8.4
) can be absorbed into the left-hand side. Hence, for any
K\geq K_{0}
, we have that

\iint\left|\partial_{nn}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{L}_{1,1/2}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left(K^{5}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+K^{-1}\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\right)\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

Combining with Step 1, we conclude that for
K\geq K_{0}
,

\iint\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{L}_{1,1/2}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}+C\left(K^{5}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+K^{-1}\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\right)\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

as desired.

8.2.
Proof of estimates in Step 1 - 5

We start with
L^{2}
square function estimates for
\nabla_{x}u
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+L
be a parabolic operator with matrix
A
in the block form (
8.1
), and let
u
be a bounded energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
. Then for
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
1\leq k\leq n-1
, and
r>0
,

(8.6)

\lambda\int_{0}^{\frac{r}{2}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dxdtdx_{n}+\frac{2}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{k}u)^{2}dxdtdx_{n}\\
\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{k}u(x,r,t)^{2}dxdt+\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{k}u(x,0,t)^{2}dxdt+C\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N^{r}(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt,

where
C
depends only on the ellipticity constant
\lambda
.

In particular, since
\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{k}u(x,r,t)^{2}dxdt\to 0
as
r\to\infty
, taking
r\to\infty
in (
8.6
) gives that

(8.7)

\lambda\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{k}u(x,0,t)^{2}dxdt+C\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right|^{2}dxdt.

Proof:
We start with a local estimate on
(0,r)\times Q_{r}
, where
Q_{r}=Q_{r}(y,s)
is a parabolic cube on the boundary as defined in (
2.16
). Let
\zeta=\zeta(x,t)
be a smooth cutoff function that satisfies

\zeta=\begin{cases}1\quad\text{in }Q_{r},\\
0\quad\text{outside }Q_{2r},\end{cases}

and that for some constant
0<c<\infty

r\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta\right|+r^{2}\left|\partial_{t}\zeta\right|\leq c\quad\text{where }1\leq i\leq n-1.

To lighten notation, we denote
w_{k}:=\partial_{k}u
for
1\leq k\leq n
. Fix a
k\in\left\{1,2,\dots,n-1\right\}
,
we write

(8.8)

\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}(\zeta^{2}x_{n})dxdtdx_{n}=\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg\left(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n}A\nabla w_{k}\right)\\
-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n}).

We then use the divergence theorem for the first term on the right-hand side, which gives us a boundary term. For the second term, we write

\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)=\nabla(\zeta^{2})x_{n}+\zeta^{2}\nabla x_{n}
, which gives two terms correspondingly. Notice that
A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla x_{n}=\partial_{n}w_{k}
thanks to the block-form structure (
8.1
), and so the corresponding term in the integral is

-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}\partial_{n}w_{k}\zeta^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{2}\zeta^{2}\right)dxdtdx_{n}\\
=\frac{1}{2}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{2}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dxdt-\frac{1}{2}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{2}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dxdt.

To summarize, the left-hand side of (
8.8
) is equal to

(8.9)

\frac{r}{2}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{2}\right)(x,r,t)\zeta(x,t)^{2}dxdt+\frac{1}{2}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{2}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dxdt\\
-\frac{1}{2}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{2}\zeta(x,t)^{2}dxdt-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}x_{n}A_{\parallel}\nabla_{x}w_{k}\cdot\nabla_{x}(\zeta^{2})-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n}).

Now we use the PDE of
w_{k}
to treat the last term. Since
\divg A\nabla u=\partial_{t}u
, a direct computation shows that

-\divg A\nabla w_{k}=-\partial_{t}w_{k}+\divg\left((\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\right),

where we have used the notation
\vec{w}:=(w_{1},w_{2},\cdots,w_{n})^{T}=\nabla u
. Therefore,

-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n})=-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{t}w_{k}(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n})+\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\divg\left((\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\right)w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\\
=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

We continue to compute

I_{1}=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}\partial_{t}\left(w_{k}^{2}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{2}\partial_{t}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}w_{k}^{2}\partial_{t}(\zeta^{2})x_{n}

because the first integral is 0. We apply the divergence theorem to
I_{2}
and note that the boundary term is

\int_{Q_{2r}}\left[(\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\cdot\vec{e_{n}}(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n})\right]_{x_{n}=r}dxdt=0

again thanks to the special structure of the matrix. So we get that

I_{2}=-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}(\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\cdot\nabla\left(w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n}\right)dxdtdx_{n}\\
=-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}(\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\cdot\nabla w_{k}\zeta^{2}x_{n}-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{Q_{2r}}(\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\cdot\nabla\left(\zeta^{2}\right)w_{k}x_{n}

We now use (
8.9
) and our computation of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
to obtain a global estimate on
(0,r)\times\partial\Omega
.

Let
\left\{Q_{2r}^{\ell}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
be a collection of disjoint parabolic cubes of sidelength
2r
that covers
\partial\Omega
. Let
\left\{\zeta_{\ell}^{2}\right\}_{\ell}
be a partition of unity subordinate to this collection, that is,
\supp\zeta_{\ell}\subset Q_{2r}^{\ell}
and
\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\zeta_{\ell}^{2}\equiv 1
on
\partial\Omega
. Moreover, for any
\ell
,

\zeta_{\ell}=1
in
Q^{\ell}_{r}
,
and for some constant
0<c<\infty

r\left|\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta_{\ell}\right|+r^{2}\left|\partial_{t}\zeta_{\ell}\right|\leq c\quad\text{where }1\leq i\leq n-1.

Note that

(8.10)

\sum_{\ell}\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta_{\ell}^{2}=0\quad\text{for }1\leq i\leq n,\quad\text{and }\sum_{\ell}\partial_{t}\zeta_{\ell}^{2}=0.

By (
8.9
), the computation of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, and (
8.10
), we can write

(8.11)

\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}\,x_{n}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}(\zeta_{\ell}^{2}x_{n})\\
=\frac{r}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{2}\right)(x,r,t)dxdt+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{2}dxdt-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{2}dxdt\\
\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{k}A)\vec{w}\cdot\nabla w_{k}\,x_{n}\,dxdtdx_{n}}_{J}.

We estimate the last term
J
using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality (recalling
\vec{w}=\nabla u
):

\left|J\right|=\left|\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}(\partial_{k}A_{\parallel})\nabla_{x}u\cdot\nabla_{x}w_{k}\,x_{n}\,dxdtdx_{n}\right|\\
\leq\left(\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}w_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}w_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}+C_{\lambda}\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N^{r}(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt.

By ellipticity, (
8.11
), and absorbing the term
\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}
to the left-hand side, one obtains that

(8.12)

\lambda\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}\leq r\int_{\partial\Omega}\partial_{n}\left(w_{k}^{2}\right)(x,r,t)dxdt+\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{2}dxdt-\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r,t)^{2}dxdt\\
+C\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N^{r}(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt.

Fix any
r_{0}>0
. We integrate (
8.12
) in
r
variable over
[0,r_{0}]
and divide both sides by
r_{0}
. Since
(\partial_{n}w^{2})x_{n}=\partial_{n}(w^{2}x_{n})-w^{2}
,

\int_{0}^{r}\int_{\partial\Omega}r\partial_{n}(w_{k}^{2})(x,r,t)dxdtdr=r_{0}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r_{0},t)^{2}dxdt-\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}^{2}(x,x_{n},t)dxdtdx_{n},

and hence

\lambda\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left(x_{n}-\frac{x_{n}^{2}}{r_{0}}\right)\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dxdtdx_{n}\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,r_{0},t)^{2}dxdt+\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,0,t)^{2}dxdt\\
-\frac{2}{r_{0}}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{\partial\Omega}w_{k}(x,x_{n},t)^{2}dxdtdx_{n}+C\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N^{r_{0}}(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt.

Truncating the integral on the left-hand side to
[0,\frac{r_{0}}{2}]
(and replacing
r_{0}
by
r
) we obtain (
8.6
) as desired.

\square

Next, we want to get a bound for the square function of
\partial_{n}u
.

Let us denote
w_{n}:=\partial_{n}u
. We separate the derivatives as follows.

(8.13)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla w_{n}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt=\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}w_{n}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt+\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{n}w_{n}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\\
=\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{n}(\nabla_{x}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt+\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{n}w_{n}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt.

The first term on the right-hand side of (
8.13
) is fine by Lemma
8.5
. So we only need to estimate the second term on the right-hand side. By the equation,

\partial_{n}w_{n}=\partial^{2}_{nn}u=-\divg_{x}(A_{\parallel}\nabla_{x}u)+\partial_{t}u,

and hence,

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{n}w_{n}\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\lesssim\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n-1}\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{i}(a_{ij}\partial_{j}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt+\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\\
\lesssim\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt+\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq n-1}\iint_{\Omega}\left|a_{ij}\right|\left|\partial^{2}_{ij}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt+\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\\
\leq\left\|\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right|^{2}dxdt+C\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla^{2}_{x}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt+\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt.

Therefore, by (
8.13
) and Lemma
8.5
, we obtain that

(8.14)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla(\partial_{n}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|\nabla_{x}u\right|^{2}dxdt+C\left\|\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}\left|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right|^{2}dxdt\\
+C\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt.

In the rest of this subsection, we take care of the term
\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt
. We begin with a square function estimate for
D^{1/2}_{t}u
.

Lemma 8.15
.

Let
u
be a bounded solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
,
u=f
on
\partial\Omega
, and
A
be in block form (
8.1
). Then there is
C>0
depending only on the dimension and ellipticity, such that for any
K\geq 1
,

(8.16)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla D^{1/2}_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla D^{1/2}_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+CK^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla(\partial_{i}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\\
+C\left(K^{4}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+K^{-2}\left\|\left|\partial_{n}A_{||}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\right)\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt+C\left\|D^{1/2}_{t}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

where
\mu_{||}
is defined in (
1.5
).
In particular, if
\iint_{\Omega}|\nabla D^{1/2}_{t}u|^{2}x_{n}dXdt<\infty
, then by Lemma
8.5
,

(8.17)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla D^{1/2}_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq C\left(K^{4}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+K^{-2}\left\|\left|\partial_{n}A_{||}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\right)\left\|N(\nabla_{x}u)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left\|f\right\|_{\dot{L}^{2}_{1,1/2}}^{2}.

Proof:
Let us denote
w:=D^{1/2}_{t}u
.
By ellipticity,

(8.18)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dXdt\lesssim\iint A\nabla w\cdot\nabla w\,x_{n}\,dXdt\\
=-\iint\divg(A\nabla w)wx_{n}\,dXdt-\iint A\nabla w\cdot\nabla x_{n}\,w\,dXdt.

For the second term on the right-hand side, we integrate in
x_{n}
and get that

-\iint A\nabla w\cdot\nabla x_{n}\,w\,dXdt=-\frac{1}{2}\iint\partial_{n}(w^{2})dXdt=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{R}w^{2}(x,0,t)dxdt\\
=\frac{1}{2}\left\|D^{1/2}_{t}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
8.18
), we use the PDE for
w
. Notice that since
A
is in block form,
A(X,t)-A(X,s)
is non-zero only in the first
n-1
rows (and columns), and so

\divg\left(\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A(X,t)-A(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla u(X,s)ds\right)=\divg_{x}\left(\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{||}(X,t)-A_{||}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\right).

Then by Lemma
7.1
,
w
satisfies the equation

(8.19)

\partial_{t}w(X,t)=\divg(A\nabla w)(X,t)+c_{0}\divg_{x}\left(\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\right).

Hence,

(8.20)

-\iint_{\Omega}\divg(A\nabla w)wx_{n}\,dXdt=-\iint\partial_{t}w\,w\,x_{n}dXdt\\
+c_{0}\iint\divg_{x}\left(\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\right)w\,x_{n}\,dXdt=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
, we integrate in
t
and use the decay of
D^{1/2}_{t}u
as
\left|t\right|\to\infty
to get that

I_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(w^{2}x_{n})dXdt=0.

For
I_{2}
, we use the divergence theorem for
x
and then break the integral into two parts:

-\frac{1}{c_{0}}I_{2}=\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\,x_{n}\,dXdt\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{s:\left|s-t\right|\leq K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\dots+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{s:\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\dots=:I_{21}+I_{22}.

The term
I_{21}
is easier so we treat it first. We write

\left|I_{21}\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\tau)d\tau}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla_{x}w(X,t)dtdX\right|\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\int_{s-\left|t-s\right|}^{s+\left|t-s\right|}\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\tau)\right|d\tau}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|dtdX\\
\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\left|s-t\right|\leq K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|dtdX,

where
M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)
is the localized maximal function defined as in (
3.45
).
We further decompose
\left|s-t\right|\leq K^{2}x_{n}^{2}
into
\bigcup_{j\geq 0}\left\{s:2^{-j-1}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}\leq\left|s-t\right|\leq 2^{-j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}\right\}
and denote it by
\bigcup_{j\geq 0}\left\{s:\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{-j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}\right\}
. Then

\left|I_{21}\right|\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j\geq 0}\int\limits_{\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{-j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|dtdX\\
\lesssim K\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}^{2}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-j/2}\fint\limits_{\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{-j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(s)\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|dtdX\\
\lesssim K\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}^{2}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}M\left(M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(\cdot)\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot)\right|\right)(t)\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|dtdX.

By Cauchy-Schwarz and the
L^{2}
boundedness of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we get that

\left|I_{21}\right|\leq CK\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}^{2}\left(\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}M\left(M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(\cdot)\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot)\right|\right)(t)^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\left(\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX\\
\leq CK\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}^{2}\left(\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\left(\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX.

Applying Cauchy-Schwarz again, we get that

(8.21)

\left|I_{21}\right|\leq CK\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}x_{n}^{3}M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}(\left|\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\epsilon\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+C_{\epsilon}K^{4}\left\|\left|\partial_{t}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta^{3}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)(x,t)^{2}dxdt,

where we have used Young’s inequality and Corollary
3.47
.

Now we turn to the term
I_{22}
. Writing
x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})
and integration by parts give that

(8.22)

2I_{22}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t}\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\cdot(\partial_{n}\nabla_{x}w(X,t))\,x_{n}^{2}dt\,dX\\
-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t}\partial_{n}\left(\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\right)\cdot\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\,x_{n}^{2}dt\,dX\\
=:I_{221}+I_{222}

For
I_{221}
, we move
\nabla_{x}
from
w
to get that it is equal to

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t}\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-3/2}\divg_{x}\Big\{\left(A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\Big\}\,ds\,(\partial_{n}w(X,t))\,x_{n}^{2}dt\,dX.

We further split it as
\left|I_{221}\right|\leq C(I_{2211}+I_{2212}+I_{2213})
, where

I_{2211}:=K^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|x_{n}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{j}{2}}\fint\displaylimits_{\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\,dtdX,

I_{2212}:=K^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|x_{n}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{j}{2}}\fint\displaylimits_{\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,s)\right|\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\,dtdX,

and

I_{2213}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|x_{n}^{2}\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-3/2}\left|\nabla^{2}_{x}u(X,s)\right|\,ds\,dtdX

The terms
I_{2211}
and
I_{2212}
can be estimated in a similar spirit using the maximal function. We start with
I_{2212}
:

I_{2212}\leq CK^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|M\left(\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot)\right|\right)(t)\,dtdX\\
\leq CK^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}M\left(\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right|\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot)\right|\right)(t)^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX\\
\leq CK^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}dX

by the
L^{2}
boundedness of the maximal function.
Using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality again and then the Carleson condition on the coefficients, we get

I_{2212}\leq CK^{-1}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\left\|\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dtdx.

The term
I_{2211}
needs a bit more care. Observe first that for almost every
(X,t)
, there holds

\fint\displaylimits_{\left|s-t\right|\sim 2^{j}K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\leq\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|M(\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot))(t).

So we get

I_{2211}\leq CK^{-1}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|^{2}M(\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}x_{n}dXdt\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\epsilon\iint\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\left\|\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(F)^{2}dtdx,

where
F(X,t)=M(\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot))(t)
. Thanks to Lemma
3.29
, we can control
I_{2211}
by the same upper bound for
I_{2212}
.

To estimate
I_{2213}
, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and get that

(8.23)

I_{2213}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}^{2}\left(\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\bigg\{\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left|\nabla^{2}_{x}u(X,s)\right|\,ds\right)^{2}dt\bigg\}^{1/2}dX.

Since
\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|s-t\right|^{-3/2}ds=4K^{-1}x_{n}^{-1}
,
Schur’s test gives that

\bigg\{\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left|\nabla^{2}_{x}u(X,s)\right|\,ds\right)^{2}dt\bigg\}^{1/2}\leq 4K^{-1}x_{n}^{-1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}.

Then by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Young’s inequality, one obtains that

I_{2213}\leq\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dt\,dX+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dt\,dX.

This completes the estimate for
I_{221}
.

We now turn to
I_{222}
. By the Leibniz integral rule,

\partial_{n}\left(\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\right)\\
=\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-3/2}\partial_{n}\Big\{\left(A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\Big\}ds\\
-\frac{2K^{-1}}{x_{n}^{2}}\left(A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,t+K^{2}x_{n}^{2})\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,t+K^{2}x_{n}^{2})\\
-\frac{2K^{-1}}{x_{n}^{2}}\left(A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,t-K^{2}x_{n}^{2})\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,t-K^{2}x_{n}^{2}),

and accordingly
I_{222}
are split into the sum of three terms, which we call
I_{2221}
,
I_{2222}
and
I_{2223}
. Observe that the term

-I_{2221}=\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\left|s-t\right|>K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left|t-s\right|^{-3/2}\partial_{n}\Big\{\left(A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\Big\}ds\cdot\nabla_{x}w(X,t)x_{n}^{2}dXdt

can be estimated similarly as
I_{221}
, which is further split into 3 terms. One can show that

\left|I_{2221}\right|\leq\epsilon\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\left\|\left|\partial_{n}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)(x,t)^{2}dxdt\\
+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{n}\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt.

The terms
I_{2222}
and
I_{2223}
are similar. We write

\frac{1}{2}\,I_{2222}=K^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{t}^{t+K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\partial_{s}A_{\parallel}(X,s)ds\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,t+K^{2}x_{n}^{2})\cdot\nabla_{x}w(X,t)dtdX\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\int_{t-K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}^{t}\partial_{s}A_{\parallel}(X,s)ds\right)\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\cdot\nabla_{x}w(X,t-K^{2}x_{n}^{2})dtdX

by a change of variable. Then we use the localized maximal function introduced in (
3.45
) and use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to get that

\frac{1}{2}\left|I_{2222}\right|\leq K^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right)(t)\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t-K^{2}x_{n}^{2})\right|x_{n}^{2}dtdX\\
\leq K^{-1}\left(\iint M_{K^{2}x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\cdot\left(\iint\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t-K^{2}x_{n}^{2})\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\epsilon\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+C_{\epsilon}\left\|\left|\partial_{t}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta^{3}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)(x,t)^{2}dxdt,

where we have used again Corollary
3.47
.
The term
I_{2223}
can be estimated almost exactly the same and one gets that

\left|I_{2223}\right|\leq\epsilon\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla_{x}w(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+C_{\epsilon}\left\|\left|\partial_{t}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\delta^{3}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)(x,t)^{2}dxdt.

Altogether, we have proved that there exists
C>0
depending only on ellipticity and the dimension, such that for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
and any
K\geq 1
,

\iint\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq\epsilon\,C\iint\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\iint_{\Omega}\left|\nabla(\partial_{i}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\\
+\left(C_{\epsilon}K^{4}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}+C_{\epsilon}K^{-2}\left\|\left|\partial_{n}A_{||}\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}\right)\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt+C\left\|D^{1/2}_{t}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

The estimate (
8.16
) follows from taking
\epsilon
small enough so that
\epsilon C<1/2
.

\square

Now we show that the integral
\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt
can be controlled as follows.

Lemma 8.24
.

Let
u
be a bounded solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
, and let
w=D^{1/2}_{t}u
. Then there is a constant
C>0
, and for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that

(8.25)

\iint_{\Omega}\left|\partial_{t}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\leq\epsilon\iint\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dtdX+C\left(1+\epsilon^{-1}\right)\iint\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dtdX\\
+C\iint\left|\nabla_{x}^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dtdX+C(1+\epsilon^{-1})\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt.

Proof:
We claim that

(8.26)

\iint\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX=\iint H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\divg(A\nabla w)x_{n}^{2}dtdX\\
-c_{0}\iint\int_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla_{x}(H_{t}\partial_{n}w)x_{n}^{2}dtdX=:T_{1}+T_{2},

where
w=D^{1/2}_{t}u
. Assuming (
8.26
) for now, we control
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
using similar techniques as the proof of Lemma
8.15
, and we omit the details from time to time to avoid redundancy.

We start with
T_{1}
.
By Cauchy-Schwarz,

\left|T_{1}\right|\leq\left(\iint\left|H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\divg A\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq\left(\iint\left|H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\Big\{\left(\iint\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dtdX\right)^{1/2}+\left(\iint\left|A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\right)^{1/2}\Big\}\\
\leq C\left(1+\epsilon^{-1}\right)\iint\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+\epsilon\iint\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3},

where in the last inequality we have used Young’s inequality and
\left|\nabla A\right|x_{n}\leq C
, which is valid thanks to (
3.36
) in Lemma
3.32
and Remark
3.17
(3).

For
T_{2}
, we split the integral in
s\in\mathbb{R}
into
\left|s-t\right|<x_{n}^{2}
and
\left|s-t\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}
and call the two terms
T_{21}
and
T_{22}
, respectively. The term
T_{21}
will be treated similarly as
I_{21}
in the proof of Lemma
8.15
:

c_{0}^{-1}\left|T_{21}\right|=\left|\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|<x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\tau)d\tau}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\cdot\nabla_{x}(H_{t}\partial_{n}w)x_{n}^{2}\,dtdX\right|\\
\leq 2\iint\int_{\left|s-t\right|<x_{n}^{2}}\frac{M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right)(s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{1/2}}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|\nabla_{x}H_{t}\partial_{n}w\right|x_{n}^{2}dtdX,

where
M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right)(s)
is the local maximal function defined as in (
3.45
). We proceed as in term
I_{21}
, that is, we decompose
\left\{s\in\mathbb{R}:\left|s-t\right|<x_{n}^{2}\right\}
into annuli
2^{-j-1}x_{n}^{2}\leq\left|s-t\right|\leq 2^{-j}x_{n}^{2}
for
j\in\mathbb{N}
, and we control each of them by the maximal function at
t
:

c_{0}^{-1}\left|T_{21}\right|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}x_{n}^{3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M(M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right)\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot)\right|)(t)\left|\nabla_{x}H_{t}\,\partial_{n}w\right|dtdX\\
\lesssim\left(\iint M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{\tau}A_{\parallel}(X,\cdot)\right)(t)^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla_{x}\partial_{n}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\right)^{1/2}\\
\lesssim\left\|\left|\partial_{t}A\right|^{2}\delta^{3}\right\|_{C}^{1/2}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\nabla_{x}\partial_{n}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C\epsilon^{-1}\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|_{C}\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt+\epsilon\iint\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt

by Corollary
3.47
and Young’s inequality. For
T_{22}
, we move
\nabla_{x}
from
H_{t}\partial_{n}w
to get

c_{0}^{-1}T_{22}=-\iint\divg_{x}\left(\int_{\left|t-s\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\right)H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)x_{n}^{2}dtdX\\
=-\iint\int_{\left|t-s\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)-\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla_{x}u(X,s)ds\,H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)x_{n}^{2}dtdX\\
-\iint\int_{\left|t-s\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{A_{\parallel}(X,t)-A_{\parallel}(X,s)}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\nabla^{2}_{x}u(X,s)ds\,H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)x_{n}^{2}dtdX=:T_{221}+T_{222}.

We have

\left|T_{221}\right|\leq\iint\int_{\left|t-s\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,t)\right|}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\right|x_{n}^{2}dtdX\\
+\iint\int_{\left|t-s\right|\geq x_{n}^{2}}\frac{\left|\nabla_{x}A_{\parallel}(X,s)\right|}{\left|t-s\right|^{3/2}}\left|\nabla_{x}u(X,s)\right|ds\left|H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\right|x_{n}^{2}dtdX.

The first term on the right-hand side can be treated similarly as
I_{2211}
in the proof of Lemma
8.15
and be controlled by

C\left(\iint\left|\nabla_{x}A(X,t)\right|^{2}M(\nabla_{x}u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}x_{n}dtdX\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{n}w\right|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\\
\lesssim\left\|\left|\nabla_{x}A\right|^{2}\delta\right\|_{C}^{1/2}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{n}w\right|^{2}x_{n}\,dtdX\right)^{1/2}\\
\leq C\left\|\mu_{||}\right\|\int_{\partial\Omega}N(\nabla_{x}u)^{2}dxdt+C\iint\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt

using Lemma
3.29
. The second term on the right-hand side can be treated similarly as
I_{2212}
and can be controlled by the same bounds as above.
We are left with term
T_{222}
, which can be treated similarly as
I_{2213}
and controlled by

\left|T_{222}\right|\lesssim\left(\iint\left|\nabla_{x}^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\left(\iint\left|\partial_{n}w\right|^{2}x_{n}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\iint\left|\nabla_{x}^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C\iint\left|\partial_{n}w\right|^{2}x_{n}.

Collecting all the estimates, we have (
8.25
).

It remains to justify (
8.26
). We write
x_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})
and
\partial_{t}=-D_{t}^{1/2}H_{t}D_{t}^{1/2}
to get that

\iint\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX=-\frac{1}{2}\iint D_{t}^{1/2}H_{t}D_{t}^{1/2}u\,\partial_{t}u\,\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})dtdX=-\frac{1}{2}\iint H_{t}w\,\partial_{t}w\,\partial_{n}(x_{n}^{2})dtdX

by moving one
D_{t}^{1/2}
to
\partial_{t}u
. By divergence theorem, we can move
\partial_{n}
to get that

\iint\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX=\frac{1}{2}\iint H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\partial_{t}w\,x_{n}^{2}dtdX+\frac{1}{2}\iint H_{t}w\,\partial_{n}\partial_{t}w\,x_{n}^{2}dtdX.

Moving
\partial_{t}
and
H_{t}
, we see that

\iint H_{t}w\,\partial_{n}\partial_{t}w\,x_{n}^{2}dtdX=-\iint H_{t}\partial_{t}w\,\partial_{n}w\,x_{n}^{2}dtdX=\iint\partial_{t}w\,H_{t}\partial_{n}w\,x_{n}^{2}dtdX,

which shows that

\iint\left|\partial_{t}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX=\iint H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)\partial_{t}w\,x_{n}^{2}dtdX.

We then use the PDE for
\partial_{t}w
, that is, (
8.19
).
Then (
8.26
) follows from moving
\divg_{x}
to
H_{t}(\partial_{n}w)x_{n}^{2}
.

\square

We now apply the Caccioppoli inequality for
\nabla^{2}(D^{1/2}_{t}u)
(Lemma
7.4
) to the first term on the right-hand side of (
8.25
) to obtain the following estimate.

Lemma 8.27
.

There is
C>0
such that

(8.28)

\iint\left|\nabla^{2}(D_{t}^{1/2}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt\leq C\iint\left|\nabla(D^{1/2}_{t}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt+C\iint\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dXdt\\
+C\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}.

Proof:
Let
w=D^{1/2}_{t}u
.
We control the integral
\iint\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}
using (
7.5
). Let
\mathcal{W}
be a Whitney decomposition of the upper half-space
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, that is, the cubes
Q\in\mathcal{W}
satisfy the properties that they are non-overlapping,

\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}Q
,
and

2\ell(Q)\leq\dist(Q,\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})<4\ell(Q)
. By (
7.5
),

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}\int_{(x,x_{n})\in Q}\int_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt\leq\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}\int_{2Q}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\\
+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}\int_{2Q}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}\int_{2Q}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}x_{n}dtdX\\
+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}\int_{2Q}\int_{\mathbb{R}}M_{x_{n}^{2}}(\partial_{\tau}\nabla A(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}x_{n}^{5}dtdX+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}\int_{2Q}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX\\
\leq C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}x_{n}+C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}M(\nabla u(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}x_{n}\\
+C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}M_{x_{n}^{2}}(\partial_{\tau}\nabla A(X,\cdot))(t)^{2}\left|\nabla u\right|^{2}x_{n}^{5}+C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}}\left|\nabla^{2}u(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX

as the family
\left\{2Q\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{W}}
has finite overlaps. By Carleson’s inequality and Lemma
3.29
,

\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}}\left|\nabla^{2}w\right|^{2}x_{n}^{3}dXdt\leq C\left(\left\|\left|\nabla A\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}+\left\|M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\nabla A(X,\cdot)\right)^{2}x_{n}^{5}\right\|_{C}\right)\int N(\nabla u)^{2}dxdt\\
+C\iint\left|\nabla w\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX+C\iint\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}x_{n}dtdX.

By perturbation theory (Lemma
3.16
) and Lemma
3.32
, we can assume that
\left|\partial_{t}\nabla A(X,t)\right|^{2}x_{n}^{5}dXdt
is a Carleson measure with a Carleson norm bounded by
C\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}
(see Remark
3.17
(3)). Then by Corollary
3.47
, we have that
\left\|M_{x_{n}^{2}}\left(\partial_{t}\nabla A(X,\cdot)\right)^{2}x_{n}^{5}\right\|_{C}\leq C\left\|\mu\right\|_{C}
.
From this (
8.28
) follows.

\square

9.
S<N
estimates in
L^{p}
for block form matrix
A

The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+L
be a parabolic operator with matrix
A
in the block form (
8.1
) that satisfies (
1.2
), (
1.3
), and (
1.4
). Let
u
be an energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
.
For any
p\in(0,\infty)
, there is
C>0
such that

\left\|S(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\left\|N(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}.

To prove Theorem
9.1
, we first derive a
S<N
estimate on sawtooth domains, which is done using cutoff functions.

Let
\Psi
be a cutoff function on
\Omega
that satisfies the following properties.

(1)

0\leq\Psi\leq 1
,

(2)

for any
p\in(0,\infty)
, the measure given by the density

(9.2)

\left(\left|\nabla\Psi(X,t)\right|^{p}x_{n}^{p-1}+\left|\partial_{t}\Psi(X,t)\right|^{p}x_{n}^{2p-1}\right)dxdx_{n}dt

is a Carleson measure on
\Omega
.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+L
be a parabolic operator with matrix
A
in the block form (
8.1
), and let
u
be an energy solution to
\mathcal{L}u=0
in
\Omega
. Let
\Psi
be a cutoff function that satisfies the following Carleson condition: the measure
\nu
given by the density

(9.4)

d\nu=\left(\left|\nabla\Psi\right|^{2}x_{n}+\left|\partial_{n}\Psi\right|+\left|\partial_{t}\Psi\right|x_{n}\right)dxdtdx_{n}\quad\text{ is a Carleson measure}.

Let
m\geq 3
be an integer. Then

(9.5)

\iint\left|\nabla^{2}u\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dXdt\leq C\left(\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}+\left\|\left|\nabla A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\right)\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\nabla u)\right|^{2}dxdt.

More precisely, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
1\leq k\leq n
, there holds

\iint\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\,dXdt\\
\leq C\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}dxdt+\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int\left|N(\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\nabla_{x}u)\right|^{2}dxdt.

Proof:
Fix
1\leq k\leq n
, we write
w_{k}=\partial_{k}u
. By the divergence theorem,

(9.6)

\iint A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla w_{k}\left(\Psi^{m}x_{n}\right)=-\iint w_{k}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla(\Psi^{m}x_{n})\underbrace{-\iint\divg(A\nabla w_{k})(w\Psi^{m}x_{n})}_{=:J}\\
=-\iint w_{k}\Psi^{m}\partial_{n}w_{k}-m\iint w_{k}\Psi^{m-1}x_{n}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla\Psi+J\\
=\frac{m}{2}\iint w_{k}^{2}\Psi^{m-1}\partial_{n}\Psi-m\iint w_{k}\Psi^{m-1}x_{n}A\nabla w_{k}\cdot\nabla\Psi+J.

For the term
J
, we use the PDE that
w_{k}
satisfies and then integrate by parts to get that

J=-\iint(\partial_{t}w_{k})w_{k}\Psi^{m}x_{n}+\iint\divg\left((\partial_{k}A)\nabla u\right)w_{k}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\\
=\frac{m}{2}\iint w_{k}^{2}\Psi^{m-1}x_{n}\partial_{t}\Psi-\iint\Psi^{m}x_{n}\left(\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right)\nabla_{x}u\cdot\nabla_{x}w_{k}-m\iint w_{k}\Psi^{m-1}x_{n}\left(\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right)\nabla_{x}u\cdot\nabla_{x}\Psi,

where we have used
\partial_{k}a_{nn}=0
because
A
is in block form (so
a_{nn}=1
).
By ellipticity, (
9.6
), and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, one obtains that

\lambda\iint\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\iint\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}+C\iint w_{k}^{2}\Psi^{m-2}\left|\nabla\Psi\right|^{2}x_{n}\\
+C\iint\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}\left|\nabla_{x}u\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}+\frac{m}{2}\iint w_{k}^{2}\Psi^{m-1}\left|\partial_{n}\Psi\right|+\frac{m}{2}\iint w_{k}^{2}\Psi^{m-1}x_{n}\left|\partial_{t}\Psi\right|.

Using the Carleson condition satisfied by
\Psi
and
A
, and absorbing the first term to the left-hand side, we have

\frac{\lambda}{2}\iint\left|\nabla w_{k}\right|^{2}\Psi^{m}x_{n}\leq C\left\|\nu\right\|_{C}\int N\left(w_{k}\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\right)^{2}dxdt+\left\|\left|\partial_{k}A_{\parallel}\right|^{2}x_{n}\right\|_{C}\int N\left(\left|\nabla_{x}u\right|\Psi^{\frac{m}{2}}\right)^{2}dxdt,

as desired.

\square

Definition 9.7

(The cutoff function
\Psi
adapted to a sawtooth domain)
.

Let
\Delta=\Delta_{r}(x_{0},t_{0})
be a (boundary) parabolic ball of radius
r
, that is,
\Delta=\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\left|x-x_{0}\right|+\left|t-t_{0}\right|^{1/2}<r\right\}
. Let
F\subset\Delta
be a Borel set. Define the sawthooth domain

(9.8)

\Omega_{a,F}:=\bigcup_{(x,t)\in F}\Gamma_{a}(x,t),

where
\Gamma_{a}(x,t):=\left\{(y,y_{n},s)\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}:\left|y-x\right|+\left|s-t\right|^{1/2}<ay_{n}\right\}
is the parabolic cone with vertex
(x,t)
and aperture
a
.
Let
\eta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
be such that
0\leq\eta\leq 1
,
\eta=1
on
[0,1]
,
\eta=0
on
[2,+\infty)
, and
\left|\eta^{\prime}\right|\leq 2
.
Let
\Upsilon\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
be supported in
B(0,10^{-3})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, with
0\leq\Upsilon\leq 1
, and
\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}\Upsilon(y,y_{n},t)dydy_{n}dt=1
. For
\ell>0
, set

\Upsilon_{\ell}^{a}(x,x_{n},t):=\frac{1}{(a\ell)^{n+1}\ell}\Upsilon\left(\frac{x}{a\ell},\frac{x_{n}}{\ell},\frac{t}{(a\ell)^{2}}\right).

We define the cutoff function
\Psi
as

(9.9)

\Psi(x,x_{n},t):=\eta\left(\frac{ax_{n}}{2r}\right)\left({\mathds{1}}_{\Omega_{3a/2,F}}*\Upsilon^{a}_{x_{n}}\right)(x,x_{n},t).

Let
k\Delta
denote
\Delta_{kr}
for any
k>0
.
By construction,
0\leq\Psi\leq 1
,
\Psi=1
on
\Omega_{a,F}\cap\left([0,\frac{2r}{a}]\times 4\Delta\right)
, and
\supp\Psi\subset\Omega_{2a,F}\cap\left([0,\frac{4r}{a}]\times 10\Delta\right)
. Moreover, we can verify that
\Psi
satisfies the Carleson condition (
9.2
), with a constant depending on
n
,
a
, and
p
.

Lemma 9.10

(Good-
\lambda
inequality)
.

For any
\nu>0
and any
\gamma\in(0,1)
,

(9.11)

\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu,\,N_{2a}(\nabla u)(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu\right\}\right|\\
\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{2a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu/2\right\}\right|.

Proof:
Fix
\nu>0
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
.

We denote
E_{1}:=\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu,\,N_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu\right\}
and

E_{2}:=\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{2a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu/2\right\}
.
Clearly,

E_{2}
is an open
subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. When this set is empty, or is all of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
estimate (
9.11
) is trivial, so we focus on the case when the set in question is
both nonempty and proper. By Vitali’s covering lemma, we can find a non-overlapping collection of parabolic balls
\left\{\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\right\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

such that
2\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\subset E_{2}
,
10\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\cap E_{2}^{c}\neq\emptyset
for each
i
, and that
E_{2}\subset\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}5\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})
. Denote
\Delta_{i}=5\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})
, and denote by
r_{i}
the radius of
\Delta_{i}
.
Set
F_{i}:=E_{1}\cap\Delta_{i}
. Note that
E_{1}\subset\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}F_{i}
as
E_{1}\subset E_{2}
. Since
2\Delta_{i}\cap E_{2}\neq\emptyset
, there exists
(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\in 2\Delta_{i}
such that
S_{2a}(\nabla u)(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\leq\nu/2
.
Observe that for any
(x,t)\in\Delta_{i}
,
\Gamma_{2a}(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\supset\left(\Gamma_{a}(x,t)\cap\left\{(y,y_{n},s):y_{n}\geq 2r_{i}/a\right\}\right)
. Therefore, for any
(x,t)\in F_{i}
,
S_{a}^{2r_{i}/a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu/2
, where
S_{a}^{2r_{i}/a}
is the square function defined for parabolic cones truncated at height
2r_{i}/a
. It follows that

\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\left(\frac{2}{\nu}\right)^{2}\int_{F_{i}}S_{a}^{2r_{i}/a}(\nabla u)(x,t)^{2}dxdt\leq\frac{C}{\nu^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iint_{\Omega_{a,F_{i}}\cap\left\{0\leq x_{n}\leq 2r_{i}/a\right\}}\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}x_{n}dxdt,

where
\Omega_{a,F_{i}}
is the sawthooth domain defined as in (
9.8
). Let
\Psi_{i}
be the cutoff function defined as in (
9.9
) for
\Omega_{a,F_{i}}
. Since
\Psi_{i}=1
on
\Omega_{a,F_{i}}\cap\left([0,\frac{2r_{i}}{a}]\times 4\Delta_{i}\right)
, we can insert
\Psi^{m}
in the above integral, where
m\geq 3
is an integer. Then we apply Lemma
9.3
to get that

\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\frac{C}{\nu^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iint\left|\nabla(\partial_{k}u)\right|^{2}\Psi_{i}^{m}x_{n}dxdt\leq\frac{C}{\nu^{2}}\int N_{a}(\Psi_{i}^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\left|\nabla u\right|)(x,t)^{2}dxdt.

Since
\supp\Psi_{i}\subset\Omega_{2a,F_{i}}\times\left([0,\frac{4r_{i}}{a}]\times 10\Delta_{i}\right)
, the support of
N_{a}(\Psi_{i}^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\left|\nabla u\right|)
is a subset of
15\Delta_{i}
. Moreover, (recalling the definition of
\Omega_{2a,F_{i}}
) for any
(x,t)\in 15\Delta_{i}
, there exists some
(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\in F_{i}
, such that

N_{a}(\Psi_{i}^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\left|\nabla u\right|)(x,t)\leq N_{2a}(\Psi_{i}^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\left|\nabla u\right|)(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\leq N_{2a}(\left|\nabla u\right|)(x^{\prime},t^{\prime})\leq\gamma\nu

by the definition of the set
E_{1}
. That is, we have that

\left|F_{i}\right|\leq\frac{C}{\nu^{2}}\int_{15\Delta_{i}}N_{a}(\Psi_{i}^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\left|\nabla u\right|)(x,t)^{2}dxdt\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|15\Delta_{i}\right|.

Summing in
i\in\mathbb{N}
, this implies that

\left|E_{1}\right|\leq C\gamma^{2}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\Delta_{i}\right|\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\Delta(x_{i},t_{i})\right|\leq C\gamma^{2}\left|E_{2}\right|.

\square

Now that we have the good-
\lambda
inequality, Theorem
9.1
can be obtained via a standard argument.

Proof of Theorem
9.1
:

Let
\gamma\in(0,1)
be determined later. For any
\nu>0
, denote
E_{\nu}:=\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:\,S_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu,\,N_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)\leq\gamma\nu\right\}
.
We compute

\left\|S_{a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}^{p}=p\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{p-1}\left|\left\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}\times\mathbb{R}:S_{a}(\nabla u)(x,t)>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu\\
\leq p\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{p-1}\left|E_{\nu}\right|d\nu+p\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{p-1}\left|\left\{N_{2a}(\nabla u)>\gamma\nu\right\}\right|d\nu.

By Lemma
9.10
and a change of variable, one has

\left\|S_{a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}^{p}\leq C\gamma^{2}p\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{p-1}\left|\left\{S_{2a}(\nabla u)>\nu/2\right\}\right|d\nu+\gamma^{-p}p\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu^{p-1}\left|\left\{N_{2a}(\nabla u)>\nu\right\}\right|d\nu\\
=C\gamma^{2}\left\|S_{2a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}+\gamma^{-p}\left\|N_{2a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\leq C\gamma^{2}\left\|S_{a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}+C\gamma^{-p}\left\|N_{a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}.

Choosing
\gamma\in(0,1)
sufficient small so that
C\gamma^{2}<1/2
, one can hide
C\gamma^{2}\left\|S_{a}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}
to the left-hand side and obtain the desired estimate.

\square

10.
N<S+A
estimates for block form matrix
A

We follow the approach of
[
40
]
to prove nontangential maximal function estimates, with some important modifications.
The arguments in the following section are motivated by the work of
[
16
]
that introduced
stopping time arguments using an entire family of Lipschitz graphs on which the nontangential
maximal function is large in lieu of a single graph constructed via a stopping time argument.
This approach is necessary as we are using
L^{2}
averages of solutions to define the nontangential maximal
function and hence the knowledge of certain bounds for a solution on a single graph provides no
information about the
L^{2}
averages over interior balls.

The arguments differ from
[
16
]
in one substantial point, namely that we do not use the “pullback” map as it destroys the block-form structure of the matrix
A
. Instead, we use an integration by parts argument involving the function constructed via the stopping time technique. Here the argument is more akin to
[
14
]
which used this method for elliptic block-form operators. We also borrow a key idea from
[
10
]
which introduced a method that avoids using maximal functions in the good-
\lambda
inequality and hence it thus implies

N<S+A
in the
L^{p}
sense directly for all
p>1
(whereas an older method required a separate argument for
p\leq 2
).

Finally, we have to modify the approach and adapt it to the parabolic setting. We are only aware of one place where
N<S
estimates have been established in parabolic settings, namely the thesis of Rivera-Noriega
[
50
]
for the Dirichlet problem (but with some errors in that proof).

As before, we shall work under the assumption that
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}
. We will only assume the
block-form structure of the matrix
A
and impose the Carleson condition on the coefficients, but distinguishing the norm in directions parallel to the boundary).

The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 10.1
.

Let
A
be a block-form matrix (
8.1
) that satisfies the ellipticity condition (
1.2
), the Carleson condition (
1.3
), and the bound (
1.4
). Let
\mathcal{L}=-\partial_{t}+\divg(A\nabla\cdot)
.

Then there exists
\delta>0
such that if

\left[\|\mu_{\parallel}\|_{C}+\left\|x_{n}\left|\nabla_{x}A\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\right]\Big(1+\left\|x_{n}\left|\nabla A\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\Big)<\delta,

then for any
p>1
,
a>0
,
f\in\dot{L}^{p}_{1,1/2}(\partial\Omega)\cap\dot{H}_{\partial_{t}-\Delta_{x}}^{1/4}(\partial\Omega)
, there exists a
constant
C=C(n,p,a,\delta,\lambda,\Lambda)>0
, such that for the energy solution
u
to (
2.27
), there holds

(10.2)

\left\|\tilde{N}(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C(1+\|\mu\|_{C}+\|x_{n}|\nabla A_{\parallel}|\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})\left[\left\|S(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}+\left\|\nabla_{x}f\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}\right]\\
+C\left\|A(\nabla u)\right\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)},

provided we know a priori that
\|\tilde{N}(\nabla u)\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}<\infty
.
Here
\tilde{N}
is the averaged version of the nontangetial maximal function.

The energy solutions
u
constructed using Lax-Milgram lemma on
\Omega
earlier a priori belong to the space
\dot{W}^{1,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})
and thus
\nabla u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})
.
In particular, this implies that

(10.3)

w(X,t):=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.675pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-4.02336pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-2.53589pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern-1.5mu{-}\mkern-7.5mu{-}$}}\kern-1.7709pt}}\!\int\!\!\!\!\!\int_{B((X,t),\delta(X,t)/2)}|\nabla u(Y,s)|^{2}dYds\right)^{1/2}\to 0

as
x_{n}\to\infty
.

Recall that we have defined the nontangential cones in (
2.19
). As we now work on just upper half-space we modify the cones somewhat and define, for a fixed background parameter

a>0
, the nontangential cone
\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)
for a boundary point
(q,\tau)\in\partial(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R})
as follows:

(10.4)

\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)=\{(X,t)=(x,x_{n},t):\,x_{n}>a^{-1}\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|\}.

There exists
a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime}
such that
\Gamma_{a^{\prime}}(q,\tau)\subset\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)\subset\Gamma_{a^{\prime\prime}}(q,\tau)
where
\Gamma
are the cones defined in (
2.19
) and thus the
L^{p}
norms of the square and nontangential maximal functions defined with respect to
\gamma
are comparable to those defined
with respect to
\Gamma
.. But (
10.4
) is better for our purposes here, as the special direction
x_{n}
is separated from all other variables. We will refer to the parameter
a
as before the aperture and

a^{-1}
we shall call the slope of the nontangential cone
\gamma_{a}
. For the remainder of this section we assume that
N
,
S
and
A

are defined with respect to the cones
\gamma_{a}
.

For a constant
\nu>0
, define the set

(10.5)

E_{\nu,a}:=\big\{x^{\prime}\in\partial\Omega:\,N_{a}(w)(x^{\prime})>\nu\big\}.

These cones, as defined currently have vertices at the boundary, but in order to define the stopping time construction we also need to define interior cones (with vertices inside
\Omega
). In the elliptic setting these interior cones
are defined by simply shifting the boundary cone, that is for
(Q,\tau)=(q,q_{n},\tau)\in\Omega

(10.6)

\gamma_{a}(q,q_{n},\tau)=(0,q_{n},0)+\{(X,t)=(x,x_{n},t):\,x_{n}>a^{-1}\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|\}.

This is the approach also taken in
[
51
]
. However, we prefer to modify this construction and consider “enlarged” cones that have an additional feature which we explain below.

Firstly, let us consider the set
S(X,t)=\{(q,\tau)\in\partial(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}):\,(X,t)\in\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)\}
. Given the way the cones are defined, it follows that

(10.7)

S(X,t)=\{(q,\tau):\,\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|<a\,x_{n}\},

which is an open parabolic ball centered at
(x,t)
of “radius”
a\,x_{n}
. We define the cone
\gamma_{a}
at
(X,t)
as the interior of the intersection of all boundary cones that originate in
S(X,t)
. Hence

(10.8)

\gamma_{a}(X,t)=\gamma_{a}(x,x_{n},t)=\mbox{int}\bigcap\{\gamma_{a}(q,\tau):\,\|(x-q,t-\tau)\|<a\,x_{n}\}.

t
x_{n}
S(X,t)
(q,\tau)
(X,t)
\gamma_{a}(X,t)
\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)

Figure 1.
Novel cones in the
(t,x_{n})
plane.

To explain this definition we compare (
10.6
) and (
10.8
). When the time variable is not present there is no difference between these definitions, and thus there is no reason to consider (
10.8
). This is due to the fact that in this case the boundary of the cone (
10.6
) is a union of straight lines of slope
a^{-1}
originating from its vertex and intersections of such cones results in a cone of the same type.

This is not the case with the time variable. In this variable the cone defined by (
10.6
) has a cusp of shape
given by the curve
a^{-1}\sqrt{|t|}
at zero.
The definition (
10.8
) removes this cusp for interior cones at
x_{n}>0
; it is easy to see that the boundary cones

\gamma_{a}(q,\tau)
that define
\gamma_{a}(X,t)
have bounded slope of their boundary in
t
-variable at height
x_{n}>0
. This slope is bounded by
\frac{a^{-2}}{2x_{n}}
(this is derived from slope of the function
a^{-1}\sqrt{|t|}
).

Thus it follows that the boundary of the cone
\gamma_{a}(X,t)
is given by the graph of a function that is Lipschitz
with Lipschitz constant
a^{-1}
in spatial variables and Lipschitz constant
\frac{a^{-2}}{2x_{n}}
in the time variable.

Moreover, our new cones (
10.8
) maintain a key property of the cones from (
10.6
), namely, that
if
(Q,\tau)
is any point (including a boundary point) and
(X,t)\in\gamma_{a}(Q,\tau)
then

\overline{\gamma_{a}(X,t)}\subset\gamma_{a}(Q,\tau).

As in
[
16
]
we now consider the map
\hbar:\partial\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
given at each
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
defined by

(10.9)

\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t):=\inf\left\{x_{n}>0:\,\sup_{(Z,s)\in\gamma_{a}(x,x_{n},t)}w(Z,s)<\nu\right\}

At this point we observe that
\hbar_{\nu,a}w(x,t)<\infty
for all points
(x,t)\in\partial\Omega
.
This is due to the fact that the
L^{2}
averages
w(Z,s)\to 0
as
z_{n}\to\infty
, as observed earlier.

Lemma 10.10
.

Let
w
be as above. Then the following properties hold.

(i)
The function
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)
is Lipschitz in spatial variables with constant
a^{-1}
. It is also
Lipschitz in time variable above a positive height and the following holds:

(10.11)

\left|\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t)-\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(y,s)\right|\leq a^{-1}|x-y|+\frac{a^{-2}}{2\min\{\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(x,t),\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)(y,s)\}}|t-s|.

for all
(x,t),(y,s)\in\partial\Omega
. In particular,

(10.12)

|\nabla\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t)|\leq a^{-1},\qquad|\partial_{t}\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t)|\leq\frac{a^{-2}}{2\hbar_{\nu,a}(x,t)}.

(ii)
The function
\hbar_{\nu,a}(w)
satisfies the following: there exists
C_{n}>0
that depends only on
n
such that
